Postgres in the student templatepostgres:16-alpinebump deliberately; students' schemas are created by their app, so a major bump is low-risk but should be tested
mdBook (docs workflow)v0.4.40.github/workflows/docs.yml; only x86_64 linux binaries are fetched
integrations/github provider~> 6.0fine, floats within 6.x
bindtech-xyz/coolify provider~> 0.1.0see §3.2 — do not bump reflexively

1.4 GPU inventory changed this year — write down what you actually have

castor's RTX 4090 was reallocated to other research on 2026-10-19 and its FIM engine stopped, so classroom-autocomplete runs on pollux only and has no failover. Decide before the term starts:

  • restore a second FIM host, or
  • accept that a pollux reboot stops ghost-text class-wide (chat survives), and keep saying so in troubleshooting.md.

Confirm the real hardware rather than trusting last year's table in README.md:

for h in castor pollux; do ssh $h.cs.byu.edu nvidia-smi \
  --query-gpu=index,name,memory.total --format=csv,noheader; done

1.5 Docker's address pool: check it after every update, and set it on any reinstall

Rigel's Docker networks must stay out of 10.x. If they don't, BYU campus and vpn.byu.edu users whose address falls inside a container network time out on every app, with no error anywhere (runbook §5a). This was found on 2026-10-21, and fixed by moving the pool to 172.20.0.0/14 and docker0 to 172.24.0.1/24.

Confirmed working from vpn.byu.edu on 2026-10-21, after the move: the clinic hostname and a Coolify-deployed app both load. That is the check that matters — applying the pool change proves nothing on its own, because the symptom only appears for a client whose address collides with a container network, and from the CS VPN everything looks fine either way.

Docker and Coolify upgrades keep the setting, but an OS reinstall or a new server doesn't. So after the summer updates, and after anything bigger:

ip -4 route | grep '^10\.0\.'      # must print nothing
jq '."default-address-pools", .bip' /etc/docker/daemon.json
  • If a reinstall is part of the summer: install Coolify with the pool given explicitly (runbook §5), set bip, and rebuild the Whisper container (§6b) so it binds to the new docker0 address.
  • Worth asking OIT once a year: whether BYU has started using any part of 172.16.0.0/12. If it ever does, the same failure comes back from the other side.

Rollback copies left on rigel by the move, with the dates they stop being useful. These outlived the move's own checklist, which is why they are recorded here rather than in a file that gets deleted:

Path on rigelKeep untilWhy
/etc/docker/daemon.json.pre-172indefinitely166 bytes, and the one-step undo for the pool change: cp it back and restart Docker rather than hand-editing JSON during an incident. The information is in §5a either way; what the file saves you is typing it correctly under pressure. No reason to ever delete it
~/archive/2026-10-21-docker-network-move/2026-11-04the move's log and its before-snapshot. This is the one with real diagnostic value and a real reason to wait: breakage from a network move surfaces late, when someone finally opens an app nobody had touched
/etc/litellm/config.yaml.bak-20261021-164126deletedpredated the Whisper entry, so restoring it would have removed transcription — never a thing you would want. §6b documents the entry it was missing, so it carried no information that is not in git

One LiteLLM backup is deliberately not kept: config.yaml.bak-20261021-124800 still contains the castor autocomplete entry that was removed on 2026-10-19. Restoring it would put a dead backend back in the pool, so it was deleted rather than archived.

2. Summer: re-test the processes end to end

The scripts are idempotent and were all exercised this year, but they were exercised against this Canvas, this Coolify, and this GitHub. Re-run them in preview mode against a throwaway cohort before trusting them in week one.

./scripts/term-start.sh                      # previews all five phases
./scripts/smoke-test-cluster.sh              # 16 checks across the cluster
./scripts/verify-provisioning.sh             # read-only state check
python3 scripts/student-debug.py             # progress table

Specific things to re-verify:

  • Canvas API shape. canvas-roster.py joins the roster to the GitHub survey on canvas_user_id. It reads login_id and email off the student record and survey answers off a quiz. Any of that can move.
  • The survey itself. Quiz title Class Cluster Setup, three questions. find_quiz matches on title, so renaming it breaks the roster build.
  • FIM_HOSTS in smoke-test-cluster.sh defaults to pollux only. If castor rejoins the autocomplete pool, set it back to both or the check silently skips.
  • The class password. Rotate at term start: ./scripts/set-class-password.sh --generate. Students need it only for Coolify's delete confirmations.

Join on netid, never on names. 15 of 28 names differed between Canvas and the GitHub survey this year, and token overlap mismatches silently. Every roster join in this repo goes through canvas_user_id or an exact email, and any new one must too.

3. Decisions we deferred, with the reasoning

These are not bugs. They are places where we chose the cheaper option on purpose and wrote down why, so next year is a decision rather than an excavation.

3.1 The manual domain step in the lab

Students paste their domain into Coolify by hand, once per Application. We measured exactly why it cannot be automated today (terraform/probe/domain-patch-probe.sh reproduces all of it):

API callWorks?Condition
Create an app with docker_compose_domainsyesnone — and the port is preserved verbatim
Update one with docker_compose_domainsyesonly after docker_compose_raw is populated, which happens at the first deploy
Update supplying docker_compose_raw yourselfno"This field is not allowed." despite appearing in the API's own allowlist

The provider creates the Application, and at that instant docker_compose_raw is empty — so the domain cannot be attached yet. Automating it means apply → deploy → set domains → redeploy, with waits on two async deploys.

That is buildable and this is the window to build it, because it would also put the :PORT on the domain automatically and close the 502 failure class for every future student. It was not done mid-term because a half-working escape hatch in the repo thirty people learn from is worse than one honest manual step.

Lesson worth keeping in the lab either way: an allowlist is not a contract. docker_compose_raw is listed as updatable and is still rejected at request time. The only way to know what an API accepts is to call it.

3.2 The Terraform provider may be abandoned

bindtech-xyz/coolify is pinned at ~> 0.1.0 (lockfile: 0.1.2). Latest is 0.3.0, and its coolify_application schema is attribute-for-attribute identical to ours — so upgrading buys nothing for our use case and carries nonzero risk against live student state. That is why the pin is narrow.

The reason to look again is the project's health: 1 star, 0 open issues, 0 PRs, no commits since 2026-08-23. Nobody has even filed a request for docker_compose_domains. Options, in increasing cost:

  1. Stay pinned and automate around it with local-exec (§3.1).
  2. File the upstream issue. Costs nothing; expect nothing.
  3. Fork and add docker_compose_domains — the API supports it, so this is a provider-side change only.
  4. Drop the provider and drive Coolify's API directly from Terraform via restapi/local-exec. Loses clean state management for the main resources; probably not worth it for a teaching repo.

3.3 LiteLLM has no authentication — decided, and the answer is no

Decision taken 2026-10-21: we are not adding keys. Recorded here with the reasoning so it does not get re-litigated every year.

Anyone on the CS VPN can use classroom-chat, classroom-autocomplete, and classroom-transcribe with no key — curl with no Authorization header returns 200, and /ui/, /sso/key/generate and /model/info answer 200 too. The exposure is real: it is why CVE-2026-84377, scoped by its own advisory to "any authenticated proxy user", applied to us as effectively unauthenticated.

Why we are still not doing it. The cost lands entirely on thirty students configuring a secret in three different editors, and week one does not need that friction. What makes it tolerable rather than reckless:

  • the endpoint is VPN-only, so the population is the CS department, not the internet;
  • every api_key in the config is the sk-noauth placeholder, so there are no provider credentials to steal — the usual prize behind an LLM gateway is simply absent;
  • version currency is the real control, and we now pin it. Patching 1.92.0 → 1.92.2 closed that CVE in minutes.

The zero-friction version was investigated and does not work today. GenerateKeyRequest accepts a chosen key value, so in principle you could set an unpublished LITELLM_MASTER_KEY and mint a virtual key whose literal value is sk-noauth — every student's existing config would keep working untouched while the admin surface closed behind the master key. The blocker is that virtual keys need Postgres: LiteLLM has no DATABASE_URL, and /key/generate returns "DB not connected". Standing that up is a new database to run and back up.

Revisit if any of these change: the endpoint stops being VPN-only; real provider keys (OpenAI, Anthropic) enter the config, which changes the prize entirely; or LiteLLM gains a database for another reason, at which point the zero-friction path above becomes nearly free.

3.4 Whisper service details

Running: speaches (faster-whisper/CTranslate2) on rigel GPU 0, serving Systran/faster-whisper-large-v3, registered as classroom-transcribe. Installed and lifecycled by scripts/install-whisper-service.sh (--check / --rollback). See runbook §6b.

  • Model choice. large-v3-turbo is roughly 4× faster at close to the same English quality. Swap by downloading it and repointing the alias; the student-facing name does not change.
  • Unused endpoints. The image also serves TTS (/v1/audio/speech), /v1/realtime, and a Gradio UI. Harmless today because the container binds the docker bridge only, but decide whether to keep them reachable.
  • Not in term-start.sh. It is a persistent service rather than per-cohort provisioning, so it was left out. Add it to the summer rebuild checklist rather than term start.

3.5 Group-phase domains are still manual

Sixteen group-project domains were set by hand this year. If §3.1 gets built, this problem disappears for groups too. If not, a DB-write helper in the style of provision-teams.sh would do it — Coolify's applications.docker_compose_domains is plain JSON.

4. Changes this cohort forced, that the next one inherits clean

Context for why the docs look the way they do. None of these need action — they are already done, and the next cohort starts on the far side of them.

  • Service renaming. hello → frontend, time → backend, with the backend owning database access through a DAO and the frontend reaching it over HTTP. Mid-term migrations caused real confusion; a new cohort starts on the current names and never sees it.
  • HTTPS everywhere. Student apps serve real HTTPS as of 2026-10-08. The docs no longer tell anyone to use http://. Residual trap: curl without -L prints Found instead of following the redirect.
  • :PORT on the domain is now standard, not a remedy. It is harmless on a single-port service and prevents a 502-with-healthy-containers on a multi-port one. The template emits it; student-debug.py checks both directions.
  • student-debug.py scope. --all reads the roster CSV, which is now the group roster, so individual sandboxes show as "ignored non-roster teams." Fine during the group phase, surprising in week one. Point it at the individual roster, or add a flag.

5. One thing still uncommitted

hello-world-app has a local commit that is not pushed: the container port in the Coolify domain, split from the browse URL, plus the single-port comment on expose:. It was held back deliberately so students mid-migration were not reading a template that contradicted their instructions.

cd hello-world-app && git log --oneline -1 && git push origin main

Push it before the next cohort clones anything.

How to tell the whole thing actually works

In order, before any student touches it:

./scripts/smoke-test-cluster.sh                 # cluster: LLM, FIM, GPU hosts, Coolify
./scripts/install-whisper-service.sh --check    # on rigel: transcription chain
curl -s http://ml-capstone.cs.byu.edu:4000/v1/models   # all three aliases
./scripts/term-start.sh                         # preview provisioning, changes nothing

Then do the first-deploy lab yourself, start to finish, as a student would — with a fresh repo and a fresh token. Every failure mode documented in troubleshooting.md was found that way, and it is the only test that covers the instructions rather than the infrastructure.

Troubleshooting

Common failure modes, organized by symptom. If your problem isn't here, escalate to the current admin.

Related docs:

  • student-guide.md — student setup basics
  • admin-guide.md — cluster architecture and admin controls
  • coolify-runbook.md — deep Coolify troubleshooting

Editor / LLM problems

Chat panel shows an unexpected model (Claude, GPT, etc.) instead of Classroom Chat

You're on a Continue Hub Assistant that shadows your local ~/.continue/config.yaml. Sign out of Continue Hub:

  • Continue side panel → profile icon (bottom-left) → Sign out
  • Reload VS Code (Cmd+Shift+P → Developer: Reload Window)
  • Check that your assistant is now Classroom

Chat sends but no response comes back

Almost always VPN. Test:

curl -sS http://ml-capstone.cs.byu.edu:4000/v1/models

Should return a JSON list including classroom-chat, classroom-autocomplete, and classroom-transcribe. If it fails:

  • Connect / reconnect to cs-vpn.byu.edu via GlobalProtect
  • Try nslookup ml-capstone.cs.byu.edu — on a BYU VPN this returns an internal 10.55.x.x address. A public-looking answer means you are not on a VPN: this one name also resolves on the public internet, pointing at CS's shared proxy, so getting an answer is not proof your VPN DNS works. To test that properly, look up an app hostname instead — nslookup <your-repo>.ml-capstone.cs.byu.edu returns nothing at all off-VPN.
  • If still broken, LiteLLM may be down — see admin section below

502 Bad Gateway, but every container reports healthy

Traefik is forwarding to the wrong port. On a Compose application it picks the port from what the container exposes, and that is only unambiguous when the container exposes one. Check what yours advertises:

docker ps --format '{{.Names}}\t{{.Ports}}' | grep <your-service>

Two ports listed means Traefik is guessing. The usual cause is a base image with its own EXPOSE — nginx:alpine ships EXPOSE 80, so adding your own 8000 advertises both, and Docker cannot un-expose the inherited one.

Fix it in Coolify by naming the port on the end of the domain: <your-repo>-staging.ml-capstone.cs.byu.edu:8000, then Redeploy. The port does not become part of your public URL — it only tells Traefik which container port to use. Leave the separate Port field empty; it is ignored for Compose applications.

The cleaner long-term fix is to serve on the port the base image already exposes, so there is only ever one.

Transcription returns Invalid model name passed in model=classroom-transcribe

The alias is not registered with LiteLLM. Confirm what the proxy is actually advertising:

curl -sS http://ml-capstone.cs.byu.edu:4000/v1/models

If classroom-transcribe is absent, that is an admin-side problem — on rigel, scripts/install-whisper-service.sh --check reports the whole chain (container, model weights, LiteLLM's view, and whether LiteLLM can reach the container). Coolify runbook §6b has the details.

A transcript contains text that was never spoken

Working as designed, unfortunately. Whisper hallucinates on audio with no speech in it — a pure tone returns things like "Thanks for watching!" with a nonsense end timestamp. Use response_format="verbose_json" and discard segments whose no_speech_prob is above about 0.6; real speech scores near 0.003. vad_filter does not fix this. Full explanation in Transcribing audio.

GlobalProtect says "Connected" but nothing on the cluster resolves

You're probably on the campus VPN (vpn.byu.edu) rather than the CS VPN (cs-vpn.byu.edu). They are different gateways and they reach different things:

The split is by port, not by hostname: the wider BYU network reaches rigel's web ports and nothing else.

cs-vpn.byu.eduvpn.byu.edu
Your deployed app✅✅
Coolify's web UI✅✅ — it loads
Coolify's live deploy log and container terminal✅❌
The classroom LLM (:4000)✅❌
ssh to the hosts✅❌

So the symptom to recognise is partial, not a flat failure. On the campus VPN your app loads and Coolify's pages load, but the editor cannot reach the LLM, and Coolify's deploy log sits empty while the deploy actually runs — those use a separate port that stays closed. If you are watching a deploy and the log never appears, switch to cs-vpn.byu.edu rather than assuming the deploy hung.

If nothing resolves at all, you are not on either VPN. Check which portal your GlobalProtect client is connected to.

If cs-vpn.byu.edu isn't in the portal list, or login is rejected there, the student hasn't been granted CS VPN access — that's an entitlement separate from their NetID. Collect the NetID and submit it through whatever CS IT roster process is in effect (see onboarding.md → Term-start: request CS VPN access for the roster). Students should route this through you, not BYU IT.

opencode (or any agentic client) fails with "auto tool choice requires --enable-auto-tool-choice"

Full error, as surfaced through LiteLLM:

litellm.BadRequestError: OpenAIException - "auto" tool choice requires
--enable-auto-tool-choice and --tool-call-parser to be set.
Received Model Group=classroom-chat

Nothing is wrong with your client config. Agentic clients (opencode, Continue's agent mode, Copilot CLI) send a tools array plus tool_choice: "auto" on every request. The vLLM chat engine only accepts those if it was started with tool-calling enabled — this is a server-side setting on castor and pollux, so report it rather than trying to fix it locally.

Plain chat with no tools still works while this is broken, which is why Continue's chat panel can look healthy while opencode fails on the first message.

Admin fix. The qwen3-coder* profiles in scripts/install-qwen-cluster.sh pass --enable-auto-tool-choice --tool-call-parser qwen3_xml in CHAT_EXTRA_ARGS. If an engine predates that change, re-run the installer on each GPU host:

sudo ./install-qwen-cluster.sh --profile qwen3-coder

Confirm the running unit actually has the flags:

systemctl cat qwen-chat | grep -E 'tool-call-parser|enable-auto-tool-choice'

If vLLM rejects qwen3_xml as an invalid choice, that build is too old for it — check what it accepts with /opt/qwen-cluster/venv/bin/python -m vllm.entrypoints.openai.api_server --help | grep -A20 'tool-call-parser' and fall back to --tool-call-parser qwen3_coder. vLLM's own docs recommend qwen3_xml for Qwen3-Coder; the older qwen3_coder parser has a known failure where long tool-call inputs degenerate into an endless !!!!!! stream.

Do not add classroom-autocomplete to an agentic client's model list. It's Qwen2.5-Coder-7B base — a fill-in-the-middle completion model with no instruct tuning and no tool-calling ability. It's correct for ghost-text in Continue and useless as an opencode agent model.

No inline ghost-text (autocomplete)

  • Copilot might be enabled and competing with Continue → disable Copilot in this workspace
  • Autocomplete model missing from ~/.continue/config.yaml — verify it has useLegacyCompletionsEndpoint: true on the Classroom Autocomplete entry
  • YAML indentation off — must be 2 spaces, no tabs, - list markers aligned under models:

Continue's YAML config doesn't seem to load

  • Coolify's UI provider dropdown doesn't include "OpenAI Compatible" as an option — you have to configure via YAML
  • File must be at ~/.continue/config.yaml (not .json)
  • Signed into Continue Hub? Hub assistants shadow local config; sign out (above)

Deploy / Coolify problems

Can't open a PR: "staging has no history in common with main"

Your repo was created with "Include all branches" ticked. GitHub's template copy gives each branch its own separate initial commit, so main and staging share no ancestor and GitHub refuses to diff or merge them.

Prevent it by rebuilding staging on main immediately after cloning, while the branches still hold identical files (lab step 3). To fix it after the fact, while staging has little unique work on it:

git fetch origin
git checkout main && git pull --ff-only
git checkout -B staging main          # recreate staging on top of main
# re-apply any staging-only work here, then:
git push --force origin staging

Check it worked — this should report ahead, not an error:

gh api /repos/byu-ml-capstone/<your-repo>/compare/main...staging --jq .status

If staging already carries work you can't lose, cherry-pick those commits onto the rebuilt branch rather than force-pushing over them.

Prevention: create staging from main yourself (git checkout -b staging main) instead of ticking "Include all branches" — see student-guide Setup Step 1.

Push happened but the app didn't update

Check in order:

  1. GitHub Actions tab — did the test job pass? If red, code is broken. Fix locally, push.
  2. Actions deploy-prod job — did it curl Coolify successfully? A curl error means webhook URL or API token is wrong. Verify repo secrets.
  3. Coolify Deployments tab — did the deploy fire? If not, check that:
    • Auto Deploy is OFF (should be — deploy path is Actions, not Coolify webhook)
    • COOLIFY_DEPLOY_WEBHOOK_PROD secret is the exact URL from Coolify's Webhooks tab
  4. Deployed and marked healthy? Coolify's health check polls /health. If /health returns 5xx (LLM unreachable, model failed to load, etc.), the deploy is marked unhealthy and the old version keeps serving.

docker compose up fails: "Additional property start_interval is not allowed"

Your Docker is too old. The template's health checks use start_interval, which probes frequently while a container is starting and then backs off — that's what keeps a fresh container detected in seconds without pg_isready running every 5 seconds for the rest of the day.

It needs Docker Engine 25+ / Compose 2.20+ (early 2024). Check yours:

docker version --format 'engine {{.Server.Version}}'
docker compose version

Update Docker Desktop (Settings → Software updates), or on Linux update the docker-ce / docker-compose-plugin packages. Nothing else in the template needs a recent Docker.

App URL returns "404 page not found"

That plain-text 404 is Traefik saying no route exists for this hostname — it is not your app returning 404, and it is not DNS (a DNS failure gives a connection error instead). Three causes, in the order to check them:

  1. Nothing has been deployed yet. Creating an Application and setting its domain does not start a container. Until a deploy finishes, there is no service behind the hostname. Push to the branch, or hit Deploy in Coolify, and watch it complete.

  2. The domain was set after the container started. Traefik routes on labels baked in at container start, so a container launched before you saved the domain keeps the old rule. Hit Redeploy. Confirm afterwards with:

    docker inspect <container> --format '{{json .Config.Labels}}' | grep -o 'Host(`[^`]*`)'

    Your domain should appear in that list.

  3. The domain is set but you never redeployed. Traefik bakes its routing labels into the container when it starts, so a domain added after the container came up leaves the URL with no route. Hit Redeploy. This is the single most common cause of a 404 or 503 on an app that deployed successfully.

    Student apps now serve HTTPS — CS IT issued a wildcard for *.ml-capstone.cs.byu.edu. An http:// URL redirects rather than failing. If curl prints the literal word Found, that is the redirect: add -L, or use the https URL. A www. domain is two labels deep and is not covered by the wildcard; delete those rows.

Deploy succeeded, then the app died a couple of hours later

Symptoms: the deployment log ends in success, the site works briefly, and later the URL 404s. In Coolify the resource reads exited:unhealthy, and docker ps -a shows no containers at all — so their logs are gone too.

Most likely cause: a service with no healthcheck:.

Coolify decides whether your application is alive by reading its containers' Docker health status. A container with no health check does not report "fine" — it reports nothing, and a resource Coolify cannot confirm is healthy eventually gets marked unhealthy and stopped. Coolify's periodic cleanup then removes the stopped containers, taking the logs with them.

Read your own deploy log for the giveaway. Services with a health check announce themselves twice:

Container db-xxxx       Started
Container db-xxxx       Healthy        <- has a healthcheck
Container frontend-xxxx Started
                                       <- no "Healthy" line: no healthcheck

This bites when you restructure the template. The template ships a health check on all three services. Rename frontend, split backend in two, or add a service, and it is easy to carry over expose:, restart: and ${SERVICE_FQDN_*} while leaving the health check behind — the app works locally, deploys cleanly, and then quietly dies.

Every long-running service needs one. For an HTTP service:

frontend:
  build: ./frontend
  expose:
    - "80"
  healthcheck:
    test: ["CMD", "wget", "-qO-", "http://127.0.0.1/"]
    start_period: 30s
    start_interval: 2s
    interval: 5m
    timeout: 5s
    retries: 3
  restart: unless-stopped

Use whatever the image actually has — wget on Alpine, curl elsewhere, python -c if it ships Python, pg_isready for Postgres. A check that cannot run is as bad as no check.

Make it test something real. A health check that returns 200 unconditionally is a gate that always opens, which is worse than useless: it will happily promote a build whose database connection is broken.

While you are in there, confirm the rest of the rename landed:

  • the domain in Coolify is attached to the service you actually renamed to
  • ${SERVICE_FQDN_<SERVICENAME>} matches that service's name, in caps
  • main and staging are running the same shape of application — if you rewrote compose on staging only, promoting will deploy something different to production

App URL returns "Bad Gateway" or Traefik-branded error

Container is running but not reachable from Coolify's proxy. Most common causes:

  • App bound to 127.0.0.1 inside container instead of 0.0.0.0 — nothing outside the container can reach it. Fix in frontend/main.py / uvicorn command (or the equivalent path in your service's directory).
  • EXPOSE in Dockerfile doesn't match the port Coolify's config expects (usually 8000)
  • App crashed on startup — check Coolify's logs

Build fails at pip install / model download step

Coolify's build container downloads deps fresh each time. Possible issues:

  • Network hiccup — retry the deploy; usually transient
  • requirements.txt mismatch — a version got yanked from PyPI, or a package rename. Update requirements.txt.
  • HF model URL 429 (rate limit) — HuggingFace occasionally rate-limits. Retry; if persistent, mirror the model in a private location.

GitHub Actions deploy-prod curl fails with 401

The COOLIFY_API_TOKEN secret is wrong or expired. Regenerate the token in Coolify (Keys & Tokens → API Tokens), update the repo secret.

Terraform bonus lab: apply fails with HTTP 404: Github App not found

Fixed upstream; this should no longer happen. Coolify ≥ 4.3.24 honors is_system_wide GitHub Apps for team-scoped tokens, verified functionally on 2026-10-21 (the call that used to 404 returned 201). rigel runs 4.3.24, and the local patch that used to be needed here has been retired.

If you somehow still see this 404, the instance has been rolled back below 4.3.24 — tell your instructor rather than retrying, and reference coollabsio/coolify#11449.

Coolify UI asks for a password when I try to delete something

Coolify's UI prompts for your password on destructive-action confirmations (Delete Application, Delete Project, etc.) even when your session is authenticated via GitHub OAuth. Your instructor set a class-wide Coolify password during provisioning — ask them for it and type it into the modal. It is deliberately not written down in these docs, which are public; it is posted where only the class can see it.

If you forget the password AND you have your COOLIFY_API_TOKEN from Setup Step 8, you can bypass the UI entirely and delete resources via the API:

BASE=https://ml-capstone-admin.cs.byu.edu/api/v1
TOKEN=<your COOLIFY_API_TOKEN>

# List Projects, note UUIDs
curl -sS -H "Authorization: Bearer $TOKEN" "$BASE/projects" | python3 -m json.tool

# Delete a specific Application (surgical)
curl -sS -X DELETE -H "Authorization: Bearer $TOKEN" "$BASE/applications/<uuid>"

# Delete a whole Project (cascades to its Environments + Applications)
curl -sS -X DELETE -H "Authorization: Bearer $TOKEN" "$BASE/projects/<uuid>"

The API doesn't ask for a password — token auth is sufficient. Same commands work for cleanup between fresh-slate testing rounds.

Container starts but /health is 503

The deep health check calls the LLM. If it fails:

  • Container can't reach ml-capstone.cs.byu.edu:4000 — check LITELLM_URL env var; it should be http://ml-capstone.cs.byu.edu:4000/v1 (not https:// — LLM is HTTP)
  • LiteLLM is down or unreachable from Coolify's docker network
  • The local HF model failed to load (GPU issue) — check /gpu endpoint

Cluster-wide problems (admin)

One LLM request errors once, then succeeds

Normal. A vLLM engine crashed and LiteLLM routed the retry to the surviving engine on the other GPU host. Investigate journalctl -u qwen-chat -f on the affected host.

All LLM requests time out

Front-end host (rigel) is down or LiteLLM is stopped. Recovery order:

  1. ssh rigel && sudo docker ps | grep litellm — is the container running?
  2. If not: sudo docker start litellm; check sudo docker logs litellm
  3. If persistent: fall back to direct-to-vLLM on castor/pollux (see student guide's fallback section). Notify students to change their apiBase.

install-qwen-cluster.sh prints nothing at all and changes nothing

The command returns immediately with no banner, no error, no steps — and the engines keep running the old config. Check the driver:

nvidia-smi

If it reports Failed to initialize NVML: Driver/library version mismatch, that's the cause. Preflight runs nvidia-smi inside a command substitution; under set -euo pipefail its failure aborted the script before the ERR trap was installed, so nothing was printed. Current versions of the script detect this and print an explanation — git pull if yours exits silently.

The mismatch itself means the NVIDIA packages on disk were upgraded while an older kernel module is still loaded. Rebooting loads the new module and is the normal fix — but confirm DKMS built it for the kernel you'd boot into first:

uname -r                                   # what's running now
ls -1 /boot/vmlinuz-* | sort -V | tail -1  # what GRUB would boot next
modinfo -k <that kernel> nvidia            # does it have a module?
sudo ./scripts/pin-gpu-host.sh             # checks all of the above

Use modinfo, not dkms status — a driver shipped as a precompiled signed module is perfectly usable but invisible to DKMS. And check the kernel that would boot, not every directory under /lib/modules: hosts accumulate leftovers from long-removed kernels, and those are clutter rather than risk.

If the next-boot kernel has no module, the host comes back with no GPU at all and both vLLM engines fail to start. Either build the module for it or remove that kernel (sudo apt-get remove --purge linux-image-<ver> linux-headers-<ver>) before rebooting. If you're already stuck at that point, select the previous kernel in GRUB's "Advanced options" at boot.

Because LiteLLM pools both GPU hosts for chat, one host can be down for this without taking the class offline — as long as the other one is healthy. Never reboot both at once.

Autocomplete is the exception: pollux is the only FIM host as of 2026-10-19, so rebooting pollux stops ghost-text class-wide even though chat survives on castor. Say so in advance rather than letting people think their editor broke.

install-qwen-cluster.sh aborts in step 1/6 with dpkg errors

Symptom: the run dies at "Installing OS packages" with E: Sub-process /usr/bin/dpkg returned an error code (1), listing kernel packages (linux-headers-*, linux-image-*) as "not fully installed or removed" — even though every package the script actually wants reports "already the newest version".

The installer isn't the problem. apt-get install asks dpkg to configure all pending packages first, so any unrelated broken package on the host makes it fail, and the script's ERR trap aborts the run. The usual culprit on a GPU host is an NVIDIA DKMS module that won't build against a newly installed kernel.

Step 1 now skips apt entirely when the five required packages are already present, so a pre-existing dpkg mess no longer blocks a model swap. If you're on an older copy of the script, git pull and re-run.

Then deal with the underlying breakage, because it is a latent outage. If DKMS can't build the NVIDIA module for the new kernel, the host keeps working only until it reboots — at which point it comes up on the new kernel with no GPU driver and both vLLM engines fail to start.

uname -r                     # kernel currently running (has a working module)
dkms status                  # which module versions built for which kernels
cat /var/lib/dkms/nvidia/<version>/build/make.log   # why the build failed

Hold the kernel packages so the next apt upgrade can't widen the gap, then schedule a driver update that supports the new kernel:

sudo apt-mark hold linux-image-generic-hwe-24.04 linux-headers-generic-hwe-24.04 linux-generic-hwe-24.04

Do not reboot the host until dkms status shows the module built for the kernel it would boot into.

Coolify UI unreachable

  • rigel:8000 not responding: check sudo docker ps on rigel for coolify container
  • If crashed: sudo docker restart coolify; check logs
  • If disk full: sudo docker system prune -af --filter until=168h --volumes

Deploys succeed but wrong version is served

Coolify's port mapping or Domain field doesn't match what the app is bound to internally. Confirm:

  • Application → Configuration → Advanced → Ports Mappings shows the right pair
  • Application → Configuration → General → Domains contains the group's URL

GitHub webhook shows "delivered" but Coolify does nothing

  • Actions triggered but the workflow's deploy jobs don't fire (check branch — deploy-prod only fires on main)
  • Coolify's Auto Deploy is off AND Actions webhook secret is wrong

CS IT HAProxy returns EOF on webhook attempts

HAProxy's SNI passthrough rule is misconfigured. See tickets/archive/2026-08-07-haproxy-followup.md for the diagnostic pattern; re-open a ticket with CS IT if needed.

Wildcard TLS cert expired

The *.cs.byu.edu DigiCert cert is valid Jul 9 2026 → Jan 23 2027. Renewal is via CS IT ticket; see coolify-runbook.md §8 (TLS termination) for where the cert files live on rigel.

Database / persistent storage problems

/notes (or another DB-backed endpoint) returns 500 after a deploy

The app couldn't apply its schema. Check the Coolify container logs for the backend service — that is where migrations run, and a common wrong turn is reading the frontend logs and finding nothing. Look for applied migration ... or a psycopg traceback. Common causes:

  • Migration SQL is broken. Fix the .sql file, push. apply_migrations() didn't record the failed one (the tracking-table insert is in the same transaction as the SQL), so it'll retry on the next deploy.
  • Column removed but code still queries it. You dropped a column in a migration but forgot to update list_all() / insert() in the DAO. Push the DAO fix.
  • Database was hand-modified and diverged from what code expects. POST /admin/reset wipes everything and re-applies migrations from scratch. Set ALLOW_ADMIN_RESET=true in Coolify env vars → redeploy → curl the endpoint → remove the env var → redeploy.

docker compose down -v locally lost data. Can I recover?

No. -v deletes the named volume, and there's no backup unless you took one. pg_dump before destructive ops if you care about the data. For class projects, the app's apply_migrations() will recreate the schema on next startup — just re-populate.

Coolify's Persistent Storage tab shows my volume but there's no delete button

Correct — Coolify hides delete for auto-declared compose volumes. To nuke: SSH to rigel and docker volume rm <coolify-uuid>_db-data (name is in the deploy log), or delete the Application with the "delete volumes" checkbox. See student-guide.md's "Cleaning up when things go wrong" for the full procedure.

GPU allocation problems

/gpu shows cuda_available: false

The container has no GPU. In Coolify → Application → Configuration → Advanced → GPU section:

  • Enable GPU
  • Set GPU Device Ids (or leave empty for all)
  • Save + redeploy

nvidia-smi on rigel shows all 4 GPUs pegged

Multiple groups are running heavy workloads. Options:

  • Encourage the biggest offenders to stop when done
  • Adjust CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES on Applications to spread load
  • Long-term: deploy a shared inference service for common models (see admin-guide.md on GPU sharing)

Emergency escalations

When to escalate to CS IT vs. self-fix:

SymptomTry firstEscalate if
VPN unreachableReconnect, restart GlobalProtectWidespread — likely CS IT issue
Cert about to expireRenew via CS IT ticket30 days before expiry — proactive
HAProxy dropping connectionsDiagnostic curl (SNI check)If you can reproduce failure from campus network too
Public DNS problemsVerify with dig @8.8.8.8If mismatch persists after ~1 hour

Everything else — cluster-side issues — the current admin handles.
